=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a string word, and an integer numFriends.
Alice is organizing a game for her numFriends friends. There are multiple rounds in the game, where in each round:

word is split into numFriends non-empty strings, such that no previous round has had the exact same split.
All the split words are put into a box.

Find the lexicographically largest string from the box after all the rounds are finished.
 
Example 1:

Input: word = "dbca", numFriends = 2
Output: "dbc"
Explanation: 
All possible splits are:

"d" and "bca".
"db" and "ca".
"dbc" and "a".


Example 2:

Input: word = "gggg", numFriends = 4
Output: "g"
Explanation: 
The only possible split is: "g", "g", "g", and "g".

 
Constraints:

1 <= word.length <= 5 * 10^3
word consists only of lowercase English letters.
1 <= numFriends <= word.length

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def answerString(self, word: str, numFriends: int) -> str:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

A partition of s into k non-empty contiguous substrings corresponds to k-1 cut positions c_1<...<c_{k-1} in {1,...,n-1}. Pieces are s[c_t:c_{t+1}] (with c_0=0,c_k=n). But is answer max over candidates with length min(M,n-i) for all i? Need prove for fixed start, longest allowed is lex max. Yes because any shorter substring is prefix of the longest allowed (since same start) and shorter prefix is lex smaller. Thus only one candidate per start. Great.

Could implement:

class Solution:
    def answerString(self, word: str, numFriends: int) -> str:
        n = len(word)
        k = numFriends
        if k == 1:
            return word
        M = n - k + 1
        best = ""
        for i in range(n):
            length = min(M, n - i)
            cand = word[i:i+length]
            if cand > best:
                best = cand
        return best

word gggg,k=4,M=1. i=0 "g"... max "g".
k=1 returns word.

```
class Solution:
    def answerString(self, word: str, numFriends: int) -> str:
        if numFriends == 1:
            return word
        n = len(word)
        size = n - numFriends + 1
        ans = ""

        return ans
```

```python
class Solution:
    def answerString(self, word: str, numFriends: int) -> str:
        if numFriends == 1:
            return word
        n = len(word)
        max_len = n - numFriends + 1
        best = ""
        for i in range(n):
            cand = word[i:i+max_len]
            if cand > best:
                best = cand
        return best
```

```python
class Solution:
    def answerString(self, word: str, numFriends: int) -> str:
        if numFriends == 1:
            return word
        
        n = len(word)
        max_len = n - numFriends + 1
        ans = ""
        for i in range(n):
            cur = word[i:i + max_len]
            if cur > ans:
                ans = cur
        return ans
```

```python
class Solution:
    def answerString(self, word: str, numFriends: int) -> str:
        if numFriends == 1:
            return word

        n = len(word)
        max_len = n - numFriends + 1
        ans = ""
        for i in range(n):
            cand = word[i:i + max_len]
            if cand > ans:
                ans = cand
        return ans
```
